with conditions:

G({\bf x}_{1,1},{\bf y}_{1,1})=G({\bf x}_{2,1},{\bf y}_{2,1})=G({\bf x}_{1,2},{\bf y}_{1,2})=G({\bf x}_{2,2},{\bf y}_{2,2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{2}}.

The space (
271
) is the space of all ordered quadruples (
8.1.3
) with the condition:

{\bf x}_{1,1}\neq{\bf x}_{2,1}
, or
{\bf y}_{1,1}\neq{\bf y}_{2,1}
; and
{\bf x}_{1,2}\neq{\bf x}_{2,2}
, or
{\bf y}_{1,2}\neq{\bf y}_{2,2}
(the internal diagonal condition).

{\bf x}_{1,1}\neq{\bf x}_{1,2}
, or
{\bf y}_{1,1}\neq{\bf y}_{1,2}
; and
{\bf x}_{2,1}\neq{\bf x}_{2,2}
, or
{\bf y}_{2,1}\neq{\bf y}_{2,2}

(the external diagonal condition for external 1-1;2-2 coordinates);

{\bf x}_{1,1}\neq{\bf x}_{2,2}
, or
{\bf y}_{1,1}\neq{\bf y}_{2,2}
; and
{\bf x}_{2,1}\neq{\bf x}_{1,2}
, or
{\bf y}_{2,1}\neq{\bf y}_{1,2}

(the external diagonal condition for external 1-2;2-1 coordinates).
The formulas (
8.1.3
), (
8.1.2
) coincide.

8.2
Stratifications

8.2.1
The stratification of the Cartesian product of two spheres
J_{1}\times J_{2}
,
dim(J_{1})=n_{1}-k_{1}
,
dim(J_{2})=n_{2}-k_{2}

Let
n_{1}
,
n_{2}
be odd,
k_{1},k_{2}
be even positive number,
n_{1}+n_{2}=n
,
k_{1}+k_{2}=k
.
This stratification is the Cartesian product of the two stratifications of the factors, described in Subsubsection
285
.
Let us define the space
J_{1}^{[s]}\times J_{2}^{[t]}
as a subspace in
J
as the union of all subspaces
J(x_{\mathfrak{k}_{1}},\dots,x_{\mathfrak{k}_{s}})\times J(y_{\mathfrak{k}_{1}},\dots,y_{\mathfrak{k}_{t}})\subset J_{1}\times J_{2}
.

Therefore, the following double stratification

\displaystyle J^{(r_{1})}_{1}\times J^{(r_{2})}_{2}\subset\dots\subset J^{(0)}_{1}\times J^{(0)}_{2},

(285)

of the space
J_{1}\times J_{2}
is well defined.
For a given stratum the number

r_{1}-s+r_{2}-t
,
2(r+s)-1=n-k
, of omitted coordinates is called the deep of the stratum.

8.2.2
The stratification of
{\mathbb{R}}\!{\rm P}^{n_{1}-k_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\!{\rm P}^{n_{2}-k_{2}}

Let us define the stratification of the product
{\mathbb{R}}\!{\rm P}^{n_{1}-k_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\!{\rm P}^{n_{2}-k_{2}}
of projective spaces is defined by the product of the stratifications of factors defined in Subsubsection (
7.2.2
).
Denote the maximal open cell

p^{-1}(J(x_{1},\dots,x_{r_{1}})\times J(y_{1},\dots,y_{r_{2}})
by

\displaystyle U(x_{1},\dots,x_{r_{1}})\times U(y_{1},\dots y_{r_{2}})\subset S^{n_{1}-k_{1}}/-1\times S^{n_{2}-k_{2}}/-1.

(286)

This cell is called an elementary stratum of the deep
0
.
A point on an elementary stratum of an arbitrary deep
(r_{1}-s,r_{2}-t)

is defined by the collection of spherical coordinates

(\check{x}_{\mathfrak{k}_{1}},\dots,\check{x}_{\mathfrak{k}_{s}},\check{y}_{\mathfrak{k}_{1}},\dots\check{y}_{\mathfrak{k}_{t}};l_{1}\times l_{2})
, where
\check{x}_{\mathfrak{k}_{i}}
,
\check{y}_{\mathfrak{k}_{j}}
are coordinates
on a circle, which is covered the circles of the join with the numbers
\mathfrak{k}_{i},\mathfrak{k}_{j}
;
l_{1}\times l_{2}
are coordinates on

(s-1)\times(t-1)
-dimensional subsimplex of the product of the joins.

8.2.3
The stratification of the polyhedron

\overline{{\bf NN}}_{\circ}
(
271
)

The polyhedron

\overline{{\bf NN}}_{\circ}
is the disjoin union
of elementary strata, which are defined as inverse images of the strata
(
286
).
Let us denote this strata by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\overline{KK}^{[r_{1}-s]}(\mathfrak{k}_{1,1},\mathfrak{k}_{2,1}\dots,\mathfrak{k}_{1,s},\mathfrak{k}_{2,s})\times\overline{KK}^{[r_{2}-t]}(\mathfrak{k}_{1,1},\mathfrak{k}_{2,1},\dots,\mathfrak{k}_{1,t},\mathfrak{k}_{2,t})=\\
\overline{KK}^{[r_{1}-s;r_{2}-t]}(\mathfrak{k}_{1,1},\mathfrak{k}_{2,1}\dots,\mathfrak{k}_{1,s},\mathfrak{k}_{2,s};\mathfrak{k}_{1,1},\mathfrak{k}_{2,1},\dots,\mathfrak{k}_{1,t},\mathfrak{k}_{2,t})\\
1\leq s_{i}\leq r_{i},i=1,2;\quad r_{1}+r_{2}=r.\end{array}

Let us describe an elementary stratum (
8.2.3
) by means of coordinate system.
For the simplicity of denotation, let us consider the case

s_{1}=r_{1}
,
s_{2}=r_{2}
this is the case of maximal elementary stratum. Analogous formula exists for points on deeper elementary strata (
8.2.3
).

Assume a quadruple of points

(({\bf x}_{1,1},{\bf x}_{1,2}),({\bf y}_{1,1},{\bf y}_{1,2});({\bf x}_{2,1},{\bf x}_{2,2}),({\bf y}_{2,1},{\bf y}_{2,2}))
determines a point on (
8.2.3
), let us fix for a quadruple a lift

((\check{\bf x}_{1,1},\check{\bf x}_{1,2}),(\check{\bf y}_{1,1},\check{\bf y}_{1,2});(\check{\bf x}_{2,1},\check{\bf x}_{2,2});(\check{\bf y}_{2,1},\check{\bf y}_{2,2}))
on the covering sphere

S^{n_{1}-k_{1}}
, each point is mapped into the corresponding point of the quadruple by the projection
S^{n_{1}-k_{1}}\times S^{n_{2}-k_{2}}\to{\mathbb{R}}\!{\rm P}^{n_{1}-k_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\!{\rm P}^{n_{2}-k_{2}}
.
With respect to constructions above, denote by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}(\check{x}_{1,1;i},\check{x}_{1,2;i}),(\check{y}_{1,1;j},\check{y}_{1;2;j});\\
(\check{x}_{2,1;i},\check{x}_{2,2;i});(\check{y}_{2,1;j},\check{y}_{2,2;j}),\\
i=1,\dots,r_{1};\quad j=1,\dots,r_{2}\\
\end{array}

the collection of spherical coordinates of the corresponding point.
A spherical coordinate determines a point on the circle
with the same number

i,j
, the circle is a covering over the corresponding coordinate

J_{1}(i)\subset J_{1}
(
J_{2}(j)\subset J_{2}
) in the join.

Recall, that a coordinate in a quadruple
with a prescribed number
i
(
j
) of the corresponding angle coordinate determines a point in a prescribed fiber of the standard cyclic

{\bf i}
-covering
S^{1}\to S^{1}/{\bf i}
.

Collections of spherical coordinates (
8.2.3
) of points
are considered with respect to common elementary transformations. A common transformation of
x,y
-collections with respect to the first and second superscripts is possible, this transformation is described by a monodromy over a closed path
and describes the representation of the fundamental group of the configuration space, which is called the structuring group. Recall, that each
{\bf x}
and
{\bf y}
coordinate of a point is defined up to undependably multiplication by
-1
. Denote by
{\bf D}{\bf D}
the group of the order
32
which is
the product of the two dihedral groups
{\bf D}{\bf D}={\bf D}_{{\bf x}}\times{\bf D}_{{\bf y}}
with a common residue class of the permutation preserving subgroup
A\times\dot{A}\subset{\bf D}
in the each factor.
Collection of spherical coordinates of a point on the configuration space is transformed up to

8\cdot 4\times 8\cdot 4\times 2=128\cdot 16
different transformations. (Up to transformation of the group
{\bf D}{\bf D}\rtimes{\bf D}{\bf D}
of the order
2048
.)

8.2.4
A classification of strata of the polyhedron

\overline{KK}_{\circ}
(
8.1.2
) by the residues collection

Elementary strata of the polyhedron (
8.1.2
),
accordingly with collections of coordinates, are divided into several types, a full description of subtypes of a type is unrequired. Let us introduce types

\displaystyle\{{\bf I}_{b},{\bf I}_{a\times\dot{a}},{\bf I}_{d}\}

(295)

of strata of the polyhedron
(
8.1.2
).

For an arbitrary elementary strata (
8.2.3
) a residues collection of pairs (a number of an
x
or
y
residues in a pair is defined by a first superscript and a pair itself in the collection is considered over a second superscript):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\{v_{1,\mathfrak{k}_{i}}=\check{x}_{1,1;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}\check{x}^{-1}_{1,2;\mathfrak{k}_{i}},v_{2,\mathfrak{k}_{i}}=\check{x}_{2,1;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\check{x}^{-1}_{2,2;\mathfrak{k}_{j}};\\
w_{1,\mathfrak{k}_{j}}=\check{y}_{1,1;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\check{y}^{-1}_{1,2;\mathfrak{k}_{j}},w_{2,\mathfrak{k}_{j}}=\check{y}_{2,1;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\check{y}^{-1}_{2,2;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\},\\
1\leq\mathfrak{k}_{i}\leq r_{1}-k_{1},\quad 1\leq\mathfrak{k}_{j}\leq r_{2}-k_{2}.\end{array}

is well defined.

The residues collection (
8.2.4
) for an elementary stratum of the polyhedron (
281
) is well defined up to
a common conjugation
\{v_{p;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}\},\{w_{p;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\}
-collection with a given superscript
p=1,2
; this transformation of resedues corresponds to one of the translations

\displaystyle({\bf x}_{p,1},{\bf y}_{p,1};{\bf x}_{p,2},{\bf y}_{p,2})\mapsto({\bf x}_{p,2},{\bf y}_{p,2};{\bf x}_{p,1},{\bf y}_{p,1}),\quad p=1,\rm{or},\-p=2;

(300)

and up to the translation
p\mapsto p+1
, which corresponds to the translation

\displaystyle({\bf x}_{1,1},{\bf y}_{1,1};{\bf x}_{1,2},{\bf y}_{1,2})\mapsto({\bf x}_{2,2},{\bf y}_{2,2};{\bf x}_{2,1},{\bf y}_{2,1})

(301)

and up to the multiplication of each element in the
\{v\}
, or,
\{w\}
-collection with a given superscript
p
by
-1
undependably.

Let
In the case residues collection for all coordinates of an elementary stratum takes values in

\{+{\bf i},-{\bf i}\}
(correspondingly, in
\{+1,-1\}
), one say that an elementary stratum is of the type

{\bf I}_{b}
(correspondingly, of the type
{\bf I}_{a\times\dot{a}}
).In the case there are at least
v
, or
w
residues in the collection of the complex type

, and of the real type

simultaneously, one shall say
about an elementary stratum of the type
{\bf I}_{d}
. In particular, in the case

,

, we get a stratum of the type

.

Let us introduce subtypes

\displaystyle\{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b},\ {\bf I}{\bf I}_{a\times\dot{a}},\ {\bf I}{\bf I}_{d}\}.

(302)

of strata of the type
{\bf I}_{b}
.
Let us introduce the collection

\displaystyle\{vv_{1;\mathfrak{k}_{i}},vv_{2;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}ww_{1;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}ww_{2;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\}

(303)

of cross-residues:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\{vv_{s;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}=\check{x}_{1,s;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}^{-1}\check{x}_{2,s;\mathfrak{k}_{i}},\\
ww_{s;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}=\check{y}_{1,s;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}^{-1}\check{y}_{2,s+1;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\},\\
s=1,2;\quad 1\leq\mathfrak{k}_{i}\leq r_{1}-k_{1},\quad 1\leq\mathfrak{k}_{j}\leq r_{2}-k_{2}.\\
\end{array}

The collections
\{v_{p;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}\},\{w_{p;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\}
,
\{vv_{s;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}\},\{ww_{s;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}\}
are not undependently, because we get a relation:

\displaystyle v_{1;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}v_{2;\mathfrak{k}_{i}}=vv_{1,\mathfrak{k}_{i}}vv_{2,\mathfrak{k}_{i}},\qquad w_{1;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}w_{2;\mathfrak{k}_{j}}=ww_{1,\mathfrak{k}_{j}}ww_{2,\mathfrak{k}_{j}}.

(308)

Apriory for an elementary strata the collection (

) is defined up to a general permutation (

), (

-multiplication.
In particular, the gauge (

Let us define subtypes (

),(

for the canonical iterated

-sheeted covering over the covering (

, because all points in the pair of

-uples (

are translated by an even number of the antipodal reflections undependently.

)

The regularization mapping (

) is defined as a covering, assotiated with the index

subgroup (

). Geometricaly, the regularization mapping is an inclusion of a subpolyhedron and is defined as the following. This geometrical regularization is a more general, because the construction is well-defined over the ambient polyhedron (

Take the projection
p_{1}\times p_{2}
(

),
i=1
, see below.

), see subsubsection

, one may assume that the images of the
{\bf x}-,{\bf y}-
-coordinate projections
p_{1},p_{2}
are inside the polyhedra (

). Take the composition with the rezolution mappings
(

) for the coordinates. We get the following mapping:

\displaystyle(p_{1}\times p_{2})\circ(\pi_{1}\times\pi_{2}):KK_{{\bf I}_{b}}\to K_{{\bf I}_{b};1}\times K_{{\bf I}_{b};2}\to S^{1}\times S^{1}=B{\mathbb{Z}}(A)\times B{\mathbb{Z}}(\dot{A}).

(326)

) and define the subpolyhedron of

-projection. The mapping (

below defines a (partial defined) "secondary"mapping:

Theorem 47
.

1. The canonical primary covering
(\ref{primcov})
over the polyhedron
KK_{{\bf I}_{d};\circ}
is a pull-back of the coordinate covering over the circles.
The formula of the circle covering is invariant with respect to the involution
\chi:({\bf x},{\bf y})\mapsto({\bf y},{\bf x})
(subsubsection
8.4.4
).

2. The canonical secondary covering
\overline{RKK}_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d}}\longrightarrow RKK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d}}
is a pull-back of the product of the two coordinate standard coverings over the circle. The
\chi
-involution. The construction is skew-invariant with respect to the involution
\chi:({\bf x},{\bf y})\mapsto({\bf y},{\bf x})
.

Proof of Theorem
47

Statements are corollaries of Lemma
45
. Apply a stabilization, described in Subsubsection
8.3.4
. We may prove the lemma with the assumption that all elementary strata admits
{\bf x}
and
{\bf y}
-residues of all different type. Let us consider the projection

p:\overbrace{KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d}}}\longrightarrow\overline{K_{{\bf I}_{d};\circ}}\subset\overline{K}_{\circ;1}\times\overline{K}_{\circ;2},

on the polyhedrons (
169
), where
\overline{K_{{\bf I}_{d};\circ,i}}\subset\overline{K}_{\circ,i}
,
i=1,2
are the subpolyhedrons, defined by the formula (
178
).
The canonical (primary) covering over
\overbrace{KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d};i}}
is the pull-back of
the coordinate canonical coverings over
\overline{K_{{\bf I}_{d};\circ,1}}\times\overline{K_{{\bf I}_{d};\circ,2}}
.
By Lemma
45
each canonical coordinate covering is the pull-back of the covering over the circle.

The construction of the covering is given by the primary residues (
8.2.4
), which are invariant with respect to the involution
{\bf x}\mapsto{\bf y}
. The formula of the residues used two coordinates primary indexes, which are invariant.
Statement 1 is proved.

Proof of Statement 2. Consider the canonical secondary covering
\overline{KK}_{{\bf I}_{b}}\longrightarrow KK_{{\bf I}_{b}}
and restrict this covering over the subpolyhedron
KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d}}\subset KK_{{\bf I}_{b}}
.
Let us apply a stabilization and decompose, analogous with the proof Statement 1, the polyhedron as following:

\displaystyle\overline{KK}_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d}}=\overline{KK}_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d};1}\cup\overline{KK}_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d};2},

(346)

where
\overline{KK}_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d};i}
,
i=1,2
is a polyhedron defined as the inverse image of the subpolyhedron
\bar{K}_{{\bf I}_{d};\circ}\subset\overline{K}_{\circ}

by the coordinate projection:

p_{i}:\overline{KK}_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d}}\to\bar{K}_{{\bf I}_{d};\circ},

using the collection of seconrary resedues and the stable condition.

Each polyhedron in the right-hand side of (
346
) is a double covering of the
projection on the first index
1
in (
166
).
This decomposition is well defined, because for
\overline{KK}_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}
a real cross-residue

vv_{1,\mathfrak{k}_{i}}
,
vv_{2,\mathfrak{k}_{i}}
is equal by (
322
). This proves that the first index
1
is global over the subpolyhedron (
346
) with external
T^{ext}
-equivariant structure (
270
). An analogous
construction for the second coordinate uses
ww_{1,\mathfrak{k}_{j}}
, or,
ww_{2,\mathfrak{k}_{j}}
cross-residues.

The canonical (secondary) covering over
\overline{KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{d};i}}
,
i=1,2
is the pull-back of
the canonical covering over
\overline{K_{{\bf I}_{d};\circ}}
; by Lemma
45
this covering is the pull-back of the covering over the circle.
The construction of the covering is given by the secondary cross-residues (
8.2.4
), which are skew-invariant with respect to the involution
{\bf x}\mapsto{\bf y}
. The formula of the cross-residues used two secondary coordinates, the involution
\chi
permutes the secondary coordinates.
Statement 2 is proved. Theorem
47
is proved. ∎

Theorem 48
.

1. There exists the
\hat{I},T^{ext}
-equivariant resolution mapping

\displaystyle\pi:\overline{KK}_{{\bf I}_{b}}\setminus\Delta\Delta_{anti}\to S^{1}\times S^{1},

(351)

which is the Cartesian product of the two coordinate mappings (

).

is even. The mapping (

) is continous in a neighbourhood of a complement of
a polyhedron
SS
of dimension

inside
\Delta\Delta_{anty}
. This polyhedron is the intersection of the deep 1-skeleton of the thick antidiagonal with the inverce image by the momentum mapping of the standard skeleton of dimension

\frac{r_{1}+r_{2}-4}{2}
in the space of momenta

\Delta^{r_{1}-1}\times\Delta^{r_{2}-1}\setminus(\Delta^{r_{1}-1}\times\Delta^{r_{2}-1})_{\circ}\simeq S^{r_{1}+r_{2}-3}.

3. Assume we have a generic polyhedron

of the codimension
2q
.
Let

be a subpolyhedron which consists of quadruples
which are inside

. Then the singular points of the restriction of the mapping (

) to
KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}(Q)
is a codimension
5q
subpolyhedron
SS(Q)\subset SS
.

Proof of Theorem
48

Proof 1. The required mapping (
351
) outside the antidiagonal is induced
from coverings over the coordinate mappings (
248
) using the stabilization (
8.3.4
) and the equivariant mappings (
347
). Let us prove that the mapping (

) satisfies the boundary conditions.

Proof 2. Take the polyhedron

. Denote by
S
the intersection of the
closures of the polyhedron with the thick antidiagonal is a 1-skeleton inside the thick antidiagonal. The image of the momentum mapping
\lambda
of

is a codimension
1
skeleton in

\Delta^{r_{1}-1}\times\Delta^{r_{2}-1}
. Denote by
K
the polyhedron in
\lambda(S)
of the dimension
\frac{r_{1}+r_{2}-4}{2}
. The dual subpolyhedron
K^{op}
of
K
in
\lambda(S)
has the dimension less by
1
. Take
\lambda^{-1}(K^{op})
, this polyhedron near the thick antidiagonal
consists of strata for which the number of cross-resedues
+{\bf i}
is stricly great that the number of cross-residues
-{\bf i}
. This means that a prescribed coordinate system in
\lambda^{-1}(K^{op})
coinsids with the nice coordinate system. The mapping

\pi
, restricted to
\lambda^{-1}(K^{op})
, is homotopic to a constant mapping.

We have proved that outside the poluhedron
S\supset SS=\lambda^{-1}(K)
the mapping
\pi
is homopopic to a constant mapping.

Proof 3. Because
Q\subset KK_{{\bf I}_{b}}
is generic, we may assume that the polyhedron
KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}(Q)
is generic with respect to a coordinate of strata of
KK_{{\bf I}_{b}}
.
This means that each cell of

is a codimension
4q
in the corresponding cell of
KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}
. This implies that

does not intersect the coordinate subjoin with
2q
coordinates. The singular polyhedron of the restriction of (
351
) on

KK_{\\
I_{b}}(Q)
near the thick antidiagonal is less then
{\operatorname{dim}}(SS)
by
q
, because we may ommite the controle over
2q
coordinates.

Theorem
48
is proved. ∎

Let us prove an analog of Lemma

Statement 2 for polyhedrons

Consider the mapping
U_{\circ}\to S^{1}\times S^{1}
, which is the restriction of
the equivariant mapping (
351
). Because the structuring group

U_{\circ}
is reduced to the subgroup
{\bf I}_{b}\times{\bf I}_{{\dot{b}}}=(b,\dot{b})
,
the mapping

U_{\circ}\to S^{1}
contains values in the even index
2\times 2
subgroup
2[\pi_{1}(S^{1})]\times 2[\pi_{1}(S^{1})]\subset\pi_{1}(S^{1})\times\pi_{1}(S^{1})
.

8.4.4
The involution
\chi

Recall
n=2^{\ell}-2
. Take the polyhedon
KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}(r_{1},r_{2})
for
r_{1}=3\cdot 2^{\ell-4}+5
,
r_{2}=3\cdot 2^{\ell-4}+5
. In particular, for
n=126
, we get
\ell=7
,
r_{1}=29
,
r_{2}=29
,

{\operatorname{dim}}(KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}})=114
.
The dimension of the singular polyhedron
SS
on the thick antidiagonal of the mapping (
351
) equals to
r_{1}+r_{2}-5=3\cdot 2^{\ell-3}+5
. In the case
\ell=7
we get
{\operatorname{dim}}(SS)=r_{1}+r_{2}-5-12=53-12=41
(because a vertical lift in the codimension
12
).
The codimension of self-intersection points of an immersed manifold in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
with the codimension
2^{\ell-3}-2
is equal to
2^{\ell-2}-4
. The codimension of
Q\subset KK_{{\bf I}_{b}}
is equals to
2^{\ell-2}-20
. The dimension of singular polyhedron
SS(Q)\subset SS
equals to
3\cdot 2^{\ell-3}+5-2^{\ell-3}+4-5/2\cdot[2^{\ell-2}-20]=19-3\cdot 2^{\ell-3}
.

This implyes that for
\ell\geq 8
the singular polyhedron
SS(Q)
is empty. In the case
\ell=7
the codimension of singular polyhedron
SS(Q)\subset SS
equals to
40
and the singular polyhedron is a curve with components inside single elementary strata. In
KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}
such a component is homotopic to a curve inside a single maximal stratun, the mapping
(\ref{Piff})
on the component is homotopic to a constant mapping.

This proves that the 2-iterated self-intersection
manifold is regular and gets not singular points of (
351
).

Take the union of the two polyhedra

KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}=KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}(r_{1},r_{2})\cup_{KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}{r_{2},r_{2})}}KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}(r_{2},r_{1}),\quad r_{1}>r_{2}.

In the case
r_{1}=r_{2}=r
we get
KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}=KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}(r,r)
.
On the polyhedron
KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}
there is the involution

\chi:KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}}\to KK_{{\bf I}{\bf I}_{b}},

which is defined by the permutation of the coordinates
({\bf x},{\bf y})\mapsto({\bf y},{\bf x})
.
Constructions of the subsection
8.3
are invariant with respect to the involution
\chi
.
